Q1. If the digit sum is \(13\) and the tens digit is \(6\), what number is formed?
A. \(69\)
B. \(67\)
C. \(68\)
D. \(66\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
6+u=13\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 67.

\[
N=10\times 6+7=67
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 67 is 13.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(67\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=67
\]

Fast result: \(67\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. Digit sum \(10\) with tens digit \(4\) identifies which two-digit number?

\[
N=10\times 4+6=46
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 46 is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(46\).

Shortcut

\[
u=6
\]

\[
N=46
\]

Fast result: \(46\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule
---
Q3. The number \(214x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(127\) and \(221\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 127 is 132.

\[
12\times 11=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 221.

\[
221=12\times 18+5
\]

\[
221-5=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{216-132}{12}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{221}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{126}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-10=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(205\) and \(313\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 205 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 313.

\[
313=18\times 17+7
\]

\[
313-7=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{306-216}{18}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{313}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-11=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q6. Between \(172\) and \(321\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 172 is 176.

\[
8\times 22=176
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 320 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 321.

\[
321=8\times 40+1
\]

\[
321-1=320
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{320-176}{8}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{321}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{171}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
40-21=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q7. Two buses leave a stand every \(90\) and \(180\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(171\)
B. \(180\)
C. \(189\)
D. \(198\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

\[
180=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(90,180)=180
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 180.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(180\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(90,180)=\frac{90\times 180}{\operatorname{HCF}(90,180)}=180
\]

Fast result: \(180\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge
---
Q8. After prime factorising \(12\), what is the number of positive divisors?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. After prime factorising \(108\), what is the number of odd divisors?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(3+1)
\]

\[
O=4
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(3+1)=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count
---
Q15. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(92\)
B. \(90\)
C. \(93\)
D. \(91\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 91.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(6415\)
B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---
Q17. For two numbers \(60\) and \(195\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(780\)
B. \(819\)
C. \(858\)
D. \(741\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
195=3\times 5\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=\frac{60\times 195}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,195)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q25. Power \(13^{33}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(33-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
13^{33}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(33-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[
6^{33}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---

Q27. When \(2^{38}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(38-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
2^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(38-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
2^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q29. Power \(2^{77}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(77-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
2^{77}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(77-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

\[
2^{77}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(47\)
B. \(45\)
C. \(46\)
D. \(44\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+9=49
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+4=94
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-9|=9\times 5=45
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-9|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\[

\]

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

\]

Q52. Between \(128\) and \(301\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 128 is 132.

\[
12\times 11=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 301.

\[
301=12\times 25+1
\]

\[
301-1=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{300-132}{12}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{301}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{127}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-10=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q56. What is the least number greater than \(376\) that is divisible by both \(15\) and \(6\)?
A. \(410\)
B. \(429\)
C. \(370\)
D. \(390\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

\[
m=30
\]

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
q=\left\lceil\frac{377}{30}\right\rceil
\]

\[
N=30\times q=390

Q88. The number \(854x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q89. Two cyclic events repeat every \(198\) and \(108\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1307\)
B. \(1426\)
C. \(1069\)
D. \(1188\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
198=2\times 3^{2}\times 11
\]

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=1188
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1188.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1188\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=\frac{198\times 108}{\operatorname{HCF}(198,108)}=1188
\]

Fast result: \(1188\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q91. Power \(2^{91}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(91-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
2^{91}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(91-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{91}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q104. Between \(120\) and \(208\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 120 is 120.

\[
15\times 8=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 195 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 208.

\[
208=15\times 13+13
\]

\[
208-13=195
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{195-120}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{208}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{119}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
13-7=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

D. \(5405\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(9\)

D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 117 is 120.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 225 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 237.

\[
237=15\times 15+12
\]

\[
237-12=225
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{225-120}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q123. When \(2^{29}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(29-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{29}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(29-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{29}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q136. The number \(936x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 26.

\[
26+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
26+x=30
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
26+4=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\[

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Final Answer

\[

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q148. Between \(102\) and \(234\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 102 is 108.

\[
12\times 9=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 228 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 234.

\[
234=12\times 19+6
\]

\[
234-6=228
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{228-108}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{234}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{101}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-8=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
N=10\times 3+1=31
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 31 is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(31\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=1
\]

\[
N=31
\]

Fast result: \(31\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q170. When \(2^{84}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(84-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{84}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(84-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

\[
2^{84}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q174. Between \(228\) and \(394\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 228 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 390 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 394.

\[
394=15\times 26+4
\]

\[
394-4=390
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{390-240}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{394}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{227}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-15=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[

\]

\]

\[

C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)

\[
S=20
\]

\[
S+x=20+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q196. The tens place has \(4\), and all digits add to \(12\). Which number is it?

D. \(47\)

\[

\]

Hence, the number is 48.

\[
N=10\times 4+8=48
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 48 is 12.

\[

Q197. Power \(7^{92}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(92-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{92}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(92-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

\[
7^{92}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q203. Between \(209\) and \(368\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 209 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 368.

\[
368=18\times 20+8
\]

\[
368-8=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{360-216}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{368}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{208}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-11=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q204. For two numbers \(63\) and \(54\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(378\)
B. \(397\)
C. \(416\)
D. \(359\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
63=3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
54=2\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,54)=378
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 378.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(378\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,54)=\frac{63\times 54}{\operatorname{HCF}(63,54)}=378
\]

Fast result: \(378\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 40! = 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(38\)
C. \(36\)
D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+7=37
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+3=73
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-7|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-7|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q224. For \(19^{44}\), use the cycle modulo \(17\). What remainder is left?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(44-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 16.

\[
19^{44}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 16.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(44-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

\[
2^{44}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q233. Digits \(8\) and \(8\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+8=88
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 8+8=88
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-8|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-8|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q237. The number \(2x092\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=13
\]

\[
S+x=13+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q255. When \(11^{61}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(61-1)\bmod 12+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
11^{61}\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(61-1)\bmod 12+1=1
\]

\[
11^{61}\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 177 is 180.

\[
12\times 15=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 273.

\[
273=12\times 22+9
\]

\[
273-9=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{264-180}{12}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{273}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{176}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-14=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\[
N=1728

Final Answer

Q288. Between \(215\) and \(372\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 215 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 372.

\[
372=18\times 20+12
\]

\[
372-12=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{360-216}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{372}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{214}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-11=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q289. Two buses leave a stand every \(126\) and \(234\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(1474\)
B. \(1638\)
C. \(1802\)
D. \(1966\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
126=2\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
234=2\times 3^{2}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=1638
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1638.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1638\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=\frac{126\times 234}{\operatorname{HCF}(126,234)}=1638
\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q291. Power \(11^{74}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(74-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{74}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(74-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
1^{74}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(35\)
B. \(38\)

D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[

\]

\[
N_2=10\times 3+7=37
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-3|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-3|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q308. Between \(121\) and \(261\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 121 is 126.

\[
9\times 14=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 261 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 261.

\[
261=9\times 29+0
\]

\[
261-0=261
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-126}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{261}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{120}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-13=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q309. For exponent \(89\), the powers of \(17\) repeat in remainders modulo \(13\). What remainder appears?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(89-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
17^{89}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(89-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
4^{89}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q312. By what least number should \(72\) be multiplied to make it a perfect cube?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=3
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q315. The number \(676x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 23.

\[
23+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,204)=2244
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(2244\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(8\)
Answer: B

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

\[
9\times |4-3|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q338. Between \(237\) and \(396\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(27\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 237 is 240.

\[
6\times 40=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 396 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 396.

\[
396=6\times 66+0
\]

\[
396-0=396
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{396-240}{6}+1=26+1=27
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 27.
Hence, there are 27 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{396}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{236}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
66-39=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{3}\to 3^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
108\times 2=216
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=216
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(216\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q348. Number \(64\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+4=64
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+6=46
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-4|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-4|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 240 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 343.

\[
343=15\times 22+13
\]

\[
343-13=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{330-240}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{343}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{239}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-15=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q365. The number \(11x96\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-3
\]

\[
-3+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-3+x=0
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-3+3=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: D

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=520
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 520.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(520\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=\frac{40\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,104)}=520
\]

Fast result: \(520\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q378. The number \(276x2\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 17; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(16\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 192 is 192.

\[
12\times 16=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 370.

\[
370=12\times 30+10
\]

\[
370-10=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{360-192}{12}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{370}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{191}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
30-15=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q392. When \(2^{39}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(39-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
2^{39}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(39-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{39}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q401. The number \(8x127\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=14
\]

\[
14-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
14-x=11
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
14-3=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q411. The number \(158x0\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 14; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

(35-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
3^{35}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(35-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
3^{35}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q421. The number \(35x70\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(504\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q424. Digits \(4\) and \(1\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(28\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(27\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+1=41
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+4=14
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-1|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-1|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q431. The number \(3x214\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 10; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
10+x=18
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
10+8=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q441. For exponent \(50\), what ending three digits does \(23^{50}\) have?
A. \(261\)
B. \(274\)
C. \(237\)
D. \(249\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
23\equiv 23\pmod{1000}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 24.

\[
23\rightarrow 529\rightarrow 167\rightarrow 841\rightarrow 343\rightarrow 889\rightarrow 447\rightarrow 281\rightarrow 463\rightarrow 649\rightarrow 927\rightarrow 321\rightarrow 383\rightarrow 809\rightarrow 607\rightarrow 961\rightarrow 103\rightarrow 369\rightarrow 487\rightarrow 201\rightarrow 623\rightarrow 329\rightarrow 567\rightarrow 41
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(50-1)\bmod 24+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 529.

\[
23^{50}\equiv 529\pmod{1000}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 529.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(249\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(50-1)\bmod 24+1=2
\]

\[
23^{50}\equiv 529\pmod{1000}
\]

Fast result: \(249\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q446. Digit sum \(14\) with tens digit \(7\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(76\)
B. \(79\)
C. \(77\)
D. \(78\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=14\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 77.

\[
N=10\times 7+7=77
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 77 is 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(77\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=77
\]

Fast result: \(77\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q447. Between \(232\) and \(368\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 232 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 368.

\[
368=18\times 20+8
\]

\[
368-8=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{360-234}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{368}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{231}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-12=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_divisibility_range_count

Q448. Two cyclic events repeat every \(88\) and \(48\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(554\)
B. \(581\)
C. \(502\)
D. \(528\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
88=2^{3}\times 11
\]

\[
48=2^{4}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=528
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 528.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(528\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=\frac{88\times 48}{\operatorname{HCF}(88,48)}=528
\]

Fast result: \(528\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[

\]

The total number of multiples is 20.

Final Answer

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
(2+1)
\]

\[
O=3
\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

\]

Q497. Between \(150\) and \(293\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)

D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 150 is 150.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 293.

\[
293=6\times 48+5
\]

\[
293-5=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{288-150}{6}+1=23+1=24
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 24.
Hence, there are 24 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{293}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{149}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
48-24=24
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q501. The number \(95x70\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q523. Between \(237\) and \(361\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 237 is 240.

\[
8\times 30=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 361.

\[
361=8\times 45+1
\]

\[
361-1=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{360-240}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{361}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{236}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
45-29=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Fast result: \(252\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q525. Power \(7^{48}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(48-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 12th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(48-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

\[
7^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q551. The number \(370x2\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=12
\]

\[
S+x=12+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q552. When \(2^{25}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(25-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{25}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(25-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{25}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q557. Between \(203\) and \(358\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 203 is 207.

\[
9\times 23=207
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 351 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 358.

\[
358=9\times 39+7
\]

\[
358-7=351
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{351-207}{9}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{358}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{202}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-22=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)

\[

\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+8=78
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-7|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q573. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q577. Tens digit \(3\) and ones digit \(5\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+5=35
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 5+3=53
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-5|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-5|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: A

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{3}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
8+2=10
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 25! is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{25}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q580. Between \(220\) and \(349\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 220 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 349.

\[
349=18\times 19+7
\]

\[
349-7=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{342-234}{18}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{349}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{219}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-12=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q581. For exponent \(107\), the powers of \(17\) repeat in remainders modulo \(13\). What remainder appears?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(107-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
17^{107}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(107-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
4^{107}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q584. The number \(32x07\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=8
\]

\[
8+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
8+x=11
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
8+3=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q585. For two numbers \(75\) and \(255\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(1275\)
B. \(1403\)
C. \(1530\)
D. \(1147\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
75=3\times 5^{2}
\]

\[
255=3\times 5\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,255)=1275
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1275.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1275\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,255)=\frac{75\times 255}{\operatorname{HCF}(75,255)}=1275
\]

Fast result: \(1275\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q605. Between \(122\) and \(220\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 122 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 210 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 220.

\[
220=15\times 14+10
\]

\[
220-10=210
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{210-135}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{220}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{121}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-8=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q606. When \(11^{82}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(82-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{82}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(82-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
4^{82}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=20
\]

\[
S+x=20+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(42,42)=42
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 42.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(42\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(42\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(51-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
13^{51}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{51}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q634. The number \(576x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 26.

\[
26+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
26+x=30
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
26+4=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: D

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(108)=280
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 280.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q644. The number \(729x5\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=19
\]

\[
19-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
19-x=11
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
19-8=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[
x=1
\]

\[

\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 126 is 126.

\[
6\times 21=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 228 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 231.

\[
231=6\times 38+3
\]

\[
231-3=228
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{228-126}{6}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{231}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{125}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
38-20=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q661. The number \(898x4\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[
11-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
11-x=11
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11-0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(90-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q668. The number \(836x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

\[
x=5
\]

\[

\[

\[

\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q682. The number \(4x650\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q683. When \(2^{53}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(53-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{53}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(53-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{53}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).
