A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=13
\]

\[
S+x=13+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q695. Between \(203\) and \(343\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 203 is 204.

\[
6\times 34=204
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 343.

\[
343=6\times 57+1
\]

\[
343-1=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{342-204}{6}+1=23+1=24
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 24.
Hence, there are 24 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{343}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{202}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
57-33=24
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[

\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
3+8=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Q719. The number \(4x214\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=11
\]

\[
S+x=11+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
11+x=18
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
11+7=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(2020\)
D. \(2244\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
132=2^{2}\times 3\times 11
\]

\[
204=2^{2}\times 3\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,204)=2244
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 2244.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2244\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,204)=\frac{132\times 204}{\operatorname{HCF}(132,204)}=2244
\]

Fast result: \(2244\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q725. Between \(175\) and \(347\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(21\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 175 is 180.

\[
9\times 20=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 347.

\[
347=9\times 38+5
\]

\[
347-5=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{342-180}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{347}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{174}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
38-19=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q736. The number \(86x49\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
7+x=11
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7+4=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-5|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q742. Between \(229\) and \(357\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 229 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 351 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 357.

\[
357=9\times 39+6
\]

\[
357-6=351
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{351-234}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{357}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{228}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-25=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q746. The number \(3x129\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[
11-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
11-x=11
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11-0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\]

Fast result: \(1092\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q766. The number \(810x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q767. Two buses leave a stand every \(88\) and \(48\) minutes. When will they next leave together?

D. \(581\)
Answer: B

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=528
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 528.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(528\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=\frac{88\times 48}{\operatorname{HCF}(88,48)}=528
\]

Fast result: \(528\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q768. When \(11^{85}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(85-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
11^{85}\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(85-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{85}\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q772. Between \(205\) and \(361\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(25\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(27\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 205 is 210.

\[
6\times 35=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 361.

\[
361=6\times 60+1
\]

\[
361-1=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{360-210}{6}+1=25+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 26.
Hence, there are 26 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{361}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
60-34=26
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q796. The number \(892x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 23.

\[
23+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q799. Between \(131\) and \(262\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 131 is 135.

\[
9\times 15=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 261 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 262.

\[
262=9\times 29+1
\]

\[
262-1=261
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-135}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{262}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{130}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-14=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q803. The number \(914x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(93-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{93}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(93-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
4^{93}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\]

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[

\]

Final Answer

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(3\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+4=44
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+4=44
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-4|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-4|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q841. The number \(176x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=9
\]

\[
9+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
9+x=11
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
9+2=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 203 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 355.

\[
355=15\times 23+10
\]

\[
355-10=345
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{345-210}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{355}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{202}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-13=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q865. The number \(9x872\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 26; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(90-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
7^{90}\equiv 9\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q868. Digits \(8\) and \(4\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(37\)
B. \(35\)
C. \(38\)
D. \(36\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+4=84
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+8=48
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-4|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-4|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q873. Between \(211\) and \(340\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 211 is 216.

\[
6\times 36=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 340.

\[
340=6\times 56+4
\]

\[
340-4=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{336-216}{6}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{340}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{210}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
56-35=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[

\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q882. The number \(51x36\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q899. The number \(214x6\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 13; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Fast result: \(612\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q901. Power \(3^{28}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
3^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q905. Between \(157\) and \(282\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 157 is 165.

\[
15\times 11=165
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 282.

\[
282=15\times 18+12
\]

\[
282-12=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-165}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{282}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{156}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-10=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\]
\[

Q923. Between \(209\) and \(375\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 209 is 216.

\[
12\times 18=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 372 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 375.

\[
375=12\times 31+3
\]

\[
375-3=372
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{372-216}{12}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{375}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{208}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-17=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q924. Power \(3^{26}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(26-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
3^{26}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(26-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
3^{26}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q933. The number \(27x92\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

(112-1)\bmod 8+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
19^{112}\equiv 1\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(112-1)\bmod 8+1=8
\]

\[
2^{112}\equiv 1\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q950. The number \(61x36\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=16
\]

\[
S+x=16+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q953. With tens \(3\) and ones \(1\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+1=31
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+3=13
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-1|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-1|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q957. The number \(976x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 26.

\[
26+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
26+x=30
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
26+4=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q963. Between \(135\) and \(282\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 135 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 282.

\[
282=15\times 18+12
\]

\[
282-12=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-135}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{282}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{134}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-8=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q967. The number \(4x872\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

Q991. Between \(149\) and \(288\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 149 is 150.

\[
6\times 25=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 288.

\[
288=6\times 48+0
\]

\[
288-0=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{288-150}{6}+1=23+1=24
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 24.
Hence, there are 24 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{288}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{148}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
48-24=24
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
p=(55-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{55}\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_cycle_length_detection

Q999. If the digit sum is \(10\) and the tens digit is \(4\), what number is formed?
A. \(47\)
B. \(45\)
C. \(48\)
D. \(46\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
4+u=10\Rightarrow u=6
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 46.

\[
N=10\times 4+6=46
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 46 is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(46\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=6
\]

\[
N=46
\]

Fast result: \(46\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_digit_formation
---
Q1000. When \(11^{69}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
11^{69}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
2^{69}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_modular_arithmetic
